## Bormul House

Bormul House was the townhouse of the patriar Bormul family of Baldur's Gate, related to the Bormul Dynasty of Amn, located in the Manorborn neighborhood.

Location
The Bormul estate stood on the crossing of two streets, the Blackraven Lane that ran parallel to the city walls and a street that moved from the Old Wall and curved eastward past the Bormul family home.

Structure
The estate was a tasteful building constructed out of yellow granite and slate. Its walls rose three stories tall. The house had a walled garden with its walls wrapped in crawling cream-colored roses. The Bormul clan had crypts underneath the townhouse.

History
In the spring of the, a distant uncle of the Bormul family visited Bormul House, bringing along a collection of curios. During his visit, the Bormul staff acted in the utmost strangest of ways, later blamed on a widely spread case of shellfish poisoning. When the uncle departed, his curiosities were left behind. Not having any storage space, the Bormul staff stashed the strange items in the family crypts underneath the townhouse and quickly forgot about them.

## Bonecloak's Apothecary

Bonecloak's Apothecary was a potionmaker's shop located in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Location
Bonecloak's Apothecary stood to the north of the Sorcerous Sundries shop in the Heapside neighborhood of Baldur's Gate. Just like the shop of wizardly goods, Bonecloak's Apothecary stood on the strake called Murl's Rest, on the crossing of Stormshore Street and Old Town Avenue.The game's city map is representative and in no way accurate to the actual layout of Baldur's Gate. This article makes an educated guess that the shop is located on the same plaza as Sorcerous Sundries, both found on the city map. Its position matches the plaza's northwestern block, which is just two buildings, the shop and the spacious Highberry family home. Its next-door neighbor was the Highberry family estate that was known to host wine festivals.

Structure

The apothecary was a flowering ivy-covered building with two floors and attic space. A small greenhouse was built into the shop's side, and it expanded into a sizable underground cave utilized to grow supplies and serve as a laboratory. The First floor was taken by the shop, and the second floor was used as living quarters by the Bonecloaks.

Services
One of the substances sold by Bonecloak's was alchemical bloodstain remover–a regular purchase delivered to the Szarr Palace.

History
In the late 15 century DR, during the Absolute crisis, Bonecloak's Apothecary was run by Derryth Bonecloak and Baelen Bonecloak, a married dwarven couple. After Baelen was left feebleminded, the business was largely run by Derryth from some half from her simple husband. She prepared concoctions and potions and regularly traveled to the Ebonlake Grotto, a friendly myconid colony in the Underdark to resupply and search for an immensely valuable noblestalk mushroom. At that time, a stray alley cat Myshka might have found a new home with Derryth in the shop. It was unknown if Baelen's mental capabilities were restored with the use of noblestalk to produce a cure.

## Books of the Master Winemaker

The Books of the Master Winemaker was an extremely valuable set of twelve books that detailed the art of growing, caring for, and harvesting of grapes and the fermentation of quality wine.

Content
Collectively, the twelve volumes were a complete guide to winemaking. The information within was detailed and guided the reader in selecting the land where a vineyard could be built, designed, and constructed; growing and tending to grapevines; and harvesting techniques. The tomes also detailed differences between varieties of grapes, the fermentation process, and various techniques and tools needed to produce the best-quality wines. Their guidance was detailed enough to offer a guide on tailoring flavors and aromas of the produced wine.

Being one of the most detailed guides to the art of winemaking, Books of the Master Winemaker was an excellent teaching tool. Diligent students could use the books to master the skill, given enough time and a winery with which to practice. They required a year of study and practice to become masters of the craft.

History
A full set of the Books of the Master Winemaker was kept at the Morden Winery near the town of Dragon Falls in the Vast, before the region suffered from the Grand Army of the Tuigan invading Faerûn and being repelled in the . A decade later, the winery was under the management of the maedar Vorlon, and over the years of running the enterprise, he learned and mastered the winemaking skill from the books. When the winery's rightful owner, Shauna Morden, reclaimed the land, a group of adventurers helped her and Vorlon come to an agreement. As one of the rewards, the heroes were granted the full set of the Books of the Master Winemaker or at least a copy of the set.

Powers
One bottle produced enough wine to emulate the effects of the cure light wounds spell. The enchantments eventually refilled the bottle once it was emptied. The same magic prohibited a person from carrying more than one bottle of the healer at the time. If a person carried more than one bottle, all the bottles immediately cracked, losing all magic and curative wine inside.

Circa the, a precious wine box of holding belonged to the maedar winemaker Vorlon who ran the Morden Winery, located in a valley outside the village of Dragon Falls in the Vast. His extradimensional box contained more treasures: an enchanted bottle of the healer and a rare crystal bottle of elverquisst bottled in ancient Myth Drannor. The maedar offered the box and its contents to the adventurers who helped him work out the deal between his winery and its rightful owner, Shauna Morden of Ravens Bluff.

## Bombard

Bombards, sometimes referred to as cannons, were a rare type of large siege engine used on spelljammers and maritime ships. The artillerists who manned bombards were sometimes referred to as bombardiers.

Description
The typical bombard consisted of a short, thick barrel that was cast from wrought iron or bronze and set within a rigid wooden base. The standard bombard was divided into two types, light and heavy. Light bombards were otherwise known as "firesticks", while heavy bombards were otherwise referred to colloquially as "hurlpits" or more formally as "farhurlers". The former derived from the fact that pits tended to be created when the stones they hurled landed. On some worlds a cannon might have a wooden frame with wheels to support it, but the bombards of Toril lacked any such gun-carriages.Such a gun-carriage is depicted in the pages of Saturday Morning Adventures II 2. Until another Realms-specific source gives a description of a bombard or cannon with wheels, we assume that the artist was in error.

Weaponology
Bombards required a crew of at least three to man them. They fell under the siege weapon category of "bombardment engines". Much like other weapons within that category, such as catapults and trebuchets, bombards were direct-fire weapons that fired their missiles in high arcs.

The missiles fired by bombards were typically large spheres of stones or cast iron. These were referred to as either "cannonballs" or "bombard shot". And on rare occasions necromancers created boneballs of skeletal undead to fire at enemy ships, which would turn into a shower of lacerating bone shards if not magically protected.

Much like other firearms, bombards required either gunpowder or smokepowder to fire. The choice of powder was dependent upon availability and the metaphysical laws of a given crystal sphere. Regardless of whichever was used, bombards consumed large, coming down to roughly ten charges of smokepowder.

The firing of a bombards was followed by a thunderously loud noise. Their rate of fire was rather slow, as they needed to be cooled and cleaned of debris after every firing. And they were subject to misfiring.

Availability

 Bombards were considered to be quite rare and expensive on both the world of Toril and across the Known Spheres. In both cases, this was partially due to bombards being viewed as rather dangerous weapons to use and the scarcity of smokepowder. On Toril, they were a danger to most ships, as firing one could send most styles of ship rolling from side-to-side. For spelljammers this "dangerous nature" was most apparent in the phlogiston where, due to its flammable nature, bombards were never used when traveling through that vacuum. Beyond Toril, their rarity was partially due to the variable nature of gunpowder and smokepowder across the Known Spheres, which made them unreliable compared to other forms of spelljammer armaments.
 The arcane, an influential race of spacefaring merchants, were known to sell bombards and smokepowder charges, the latter of which they typically sold for 3,000 gp. They often threw in two to five charges for free to anyone that bought a bombard from them.
 The Smiths' Coster trading company, who sold various firearms across wildspace.
 Beyond the Prime Material plane, bombards could be found in some parts of the Domains of Dread, though they were considered to be fairly unreliable. Unlike the bombards of the Prime, these weapons were not cast, but rather fashioned from wood or metal strips and bound together wlth iron rings.More specifically, bombards were found in Domains of Dread where the cultural level was Chivalric.

Varieties

Due to their size and crudeness, instances of magical bombards were incredibly rare, though slightly more common in wildspace.
 Great bombards, a variety of spelljammers unique to the giff, were so named for being armed with single massive bombard made of cold iron and banded with steel rings. This bombard replaced the spelljammer's prow and ran half the length of the entire ship. Unlike normal bombards, they required twenty charges of smokepowder for a single shot.
 Inferno bombards, a pair of experimental bombards that were invented in the 15 century DR by the fire giant warlord Baron Ugarak and whose construction were overseen by the azer smith Hadutha. Rather than firing balls of stone or iron, these bombards were designed to fire huge globs of elemental fire. They were ultimately sabotaged by a group of adventurers before they could be tested in battle against the town of Secomber.
 Thayan bombards, a variety of bombards that were created by the Red Wizards of Thay sometime after having learned of the developments being made by the Lantanna. They were far larger and more powerful than standard bombards. They did not require smokepowder for their propulsion and functioning, but rather some arcane means known only to the Red Wizards.

History
The first recorded usage of bombards by the people of Lantan occurred early in the . These early bombards used gunpowder, as the substance had not yet been made inert on Toril and because the Lantanna had yet to discover and perfect the recipe for smokepowder.

The wizard Elminster felt this development was significant enough to warrant arranging a private meeting with Storm Silverhand to discuss them. Through the use of his magic he showed a visual display of the bombard testing and mused that they would likely be put to use in mainland Faerûn before the year was out. Storm reasoned that it was likely rulers throughout the Realms would send mages to either capture or kill the makers of this new weapon, then asked if she should slay them first, but Elminster admonished that he already asked Mystra and she forbade it. Instead, Elminster instructed Storm to travel with the Harper Aumarra Kethtan to Lantan and protect its people from any would-be "murderous capturing mages".

Meanwhile, Thayan spies had also observed the tests being conducted in Lantan and reported back to their Red Wizard masters. The Red Wizards determined that it was crucial for them to have command of such weapons themselves, beginning development on what would become the Thayan bombard.

After gunpowder was rendered inert in 1356 DR, all further developments in standard bombards would be done with smokepowder. And despite Elminster's concerns, the Lantanna still had yet to export their bombards to mainland Faerûn by 1358 DR. This was potentially due in part to the fact that bombards, along with many other inventions of the Lantanna, were of concern to both the Harpers organization and the nation of Halruaa due to their great potential for destruction. Both groups heavily monitored the island nation, with the Halruaans observing from high above in their skyships. The Thayan bombard as well did not see much action outside of Thay itself, due in part to the Red Wizards wanting to maintain secrecy with their weapon.

By the 15 century DR, the usage of bombards on Toril remained in a state of relative rarity. This was due to gun-carriages being uncommon, which made bombards dangerous to use on most ships, and shortages in smokepowder in the wake of the Spellplague.

Notable Users
Buildings, Nations, & Settlements
 The castle Greatgard was rumored to have bombards.
 The Navy of Waterdeep was outfitted with bombards.
Individuals
 The Sea Sprite, a caravel captained by Deudermont, was outfitted with cannons.
 The legendary sentient ship Spelljammer was rumored to be outfitted with twenty bombards.
Races & Sentient Creatures
 The spacefaring giff highly favored the use of bombards. They were one of two races most responsible for proliferating bombards throughout wildspace.
 Whenever lizardfolk manned hammerships within crystal spheres where smokepowder was readily available, they were liable to outfit them with bombards in place of the standard ballistae and catapults.
 The scro, a highly advanced breed of orc feared throughout the crystal spheres, was also known to utilize bombards.
 Tinker gnomes, a variety of gnome that originated from the world of Krynn, highly favored the use of bombards and were the other race most responsible for proliferating them throughout wildspace.
Spelljammers
 Armadas, the largest of elven-made spelljammers, were sometimes outfitted with three bombards. Their predecessor, the monarch armada, were typically outfitted with two.
 Some configuration of dragonflies were outfitted with a bombard.
 Dragonships were frequently outfitted with bombards.
 Firevipers, a heavily armed modification of viperships, were typically outfitted with two bombards in place of ballistae.
 Tsunamis, the largest of the spelljammers that operated in Realmspace, were typically outfitted with six bombards.

## Borin

Borin was a trapper from the town of Mossbridges in the Vast, who ran a modest trapping business and traded with the Merchant House alongside his brothers in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Like his siblings, Borin had short-cropped brown hair and brown eyes.

Relationships
Borin had two brothers, Golin, and the youngest among the three, Chad. The brothers were acquainted with the retired half-orc fighter named Chester Hearth, who frequented the Golden Goblet Inn in Mossbridges. They considered the fighter to be a talented trapper and thought him to be a fine fellow despite being a half-orc. At one point, Borin and his siblings' dog was denied breeding with Chester Hearth's powerful moorhound.

History
When the Golden Goblet Inn was opened in the late 14 century DR, after the war in the Vast, Borin and his brothers became frequent patrons of Georgio's fine establishment. The family even celebrated Chad's 20 birthday at the Goblet.

## Bonesaw

Bonesaw was a surgical tool occasionally used as a weapon, that possessed a minor enchantment.

Powers
Some bonesaws bore a simple enchantment, similar to that of a +1 weapon that made it more deadly and easier to aim with. They came in a variety of designs. Some had sizable sharp teeth held in place by beautifully filigreed metalwork.

History
In the late 15 century DR, several of the bonesaws could be found in the House of Healing in Reithwin and in the hands of undead nurse Sister Hunna who served depraved Sharran surgeon Malus Thorm. Further north, Arla Razortongue of Luskan carried bonesaws.

## Boots of very fast blinking

Boots of very fast blinking were enchanted boots that harnessed a less useful version of the misty step spell.

Powers
Boots of very fast blinking allowed the individual wearing to blink from one place to another, like with the use of misty step. However, the spell only transported the wearer of the boots, but not their possessions nor clothes.

History
In the, a pair of boots of very fast blinking was displayed among splendorous enchanted rewards for those willing to partake in Akabi's Wheel of Fortune game in the Circus of the Last Days during its stay in the Rivington neighborhood of Baldur's Gate.

## Bowl

Bowls were a common piece of crockery found everywhere in Faerûn.

Description
Bowls were a fundamental part of kitchens and dining rooms for preparing and serving meals (often with a spoon), especially those like soups, stews, and porridge, and a standard part of the mess kit used by travelers. Cheap, common bowls were made from wood, but finer crockery was made of ceramics such as porcelain, often with decorative glazing that made them into works of art.

Mortars were special bowls used for grinding materials into powder, while braziers were essentially large metal bowls used for holding burning fuel.

Notable Bowls
 Bowl of blood, a magic bowl that produced blood.
 Bowl of commanding water elementals, a magical bowl that controlled water elementals.
 Bowl of watery death, a variety of magical bowls that looked identical to a bowl of commanding water elementals but were actually deadly traps for anyone that dared to use them.

## Bolt (fastener)

Bolts and nuts were metal fasteners that were used in metalworking, carpentry, and construction. They belonged to a broad class of hardware that included nails, spikes, and pegs.

Description
Bolts were typically less than  long and  or less in diameter. Their most important feature was their thread, which allowed a nut to attach and hold the materials in place.

A similar, simpler fastener was the link bolt, a clevis pin that had no thread and instead used an eyehole and pin to hold the rod in place, often tightened with the insertion of shims.

Availability

Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue offered a selection of bolts, nuts, and related tools in various configurations and sizes. Bolts were available from  long and able to be cut to length, in ¼", ½", ¾", and  diameters for 3 gp/lb, and with fine and coarse threads. Variously-fitted nuts were available for 2 gp/lb.

For the most part, around the 1370s DR finely-made threaded screws and bolts were treated as "holy secrets of Gond" and used in temples, furniture, and devices built and owned by that faith. Link bolts were far more common, with human, dwarf, and gnome smiths producing them widely across the Sword Coast and Shining Sea of western Faerûn.

## Bow and arrow set (play)

A bow and arrow set was a type of toy designed for children to play pretend and emulate rangers, as well as means by which to teach children proper etiquette regarding ranged weaponry. Despite being supposedly harmless fun, it was generally advised that adults supervise children playing with them.

Description
Each set consisted of three bowstrings, five arrows whose tips were padded tips by affixed bean bags, and a small bow. These bows were crafted with a very resilient willow stem, had a  pull, and weighed around .

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold bow and arrow sets made in the Dalelands for 2 gold pieces.
 The Toysmiths, a toyshop in Ravens Bluff that was owned and operated by the gnome brothers Gunder and Relvan Gaewilder, sold bow and arrow sets at the same price.

## Boots of genial striding

Boots of genial striding were a pair of enchanted boots that allowed the wearer to move unhindered by terrain.

Powers
These boots' magic was evident when one examined the soles. They warped and shifted akin to clay being molded by a potter. This magic was the enchantment similar to the constant effects of the freedom of movement spell that allowed the wearer to easily stride across even the most difficult of terrains.

History
In the, a pair of boots of genial striding belonged to the orog Blurg of the Society of Brilliance, during his stay within a friendly myconid colony of the Ebonlake Grotto in the Underdark.

## Bonedark Hills

The Bonedark Hills was the name given by the people of Thesk to a bleak and gray region of badlands in eastern Thesk and western Thay created by the upheavals of the Spellplague. The area was known to the Thayans as the Contested Lands.

Description
The land was broken by the rising and eruption of the Thaymount, resulting in scree fields and steep terrain riddled with twisting passes and tunnels. The region was covered by clouds of volcanic smoke and blanketed in ash. The sun was often not visible through the haze, and even when it was, the sheer cliffs of the looming Thayan Plateau to the east blocked the sunrise for several hours, casting the land in shadow. Travel through the area could be difficult and exhausting.

Geography
The Bonedark Hills were a broad area across eastern Thesk extending into Thay's political boundaries down the western base of the Thayan Plateau. Precarious switchback trails led up the jagged, nearly vertical cliffs of the Plateau to Thay proper, with tax stations, guard posts, and arcane siege engines protecting the routes.

History
The region was formed during the apocalyptic events of the Spellplague in the, and left the towns of Nethentir, Nethjet, and Surcross broken. In the century that followed, Thayan forces would begin to fortify these towns, much to the concern of Thesk. By the late 15 century DR, the Thayans—at the behest of Szass Tam—had begun transforming these forts into Dread Rings and his agents continually tested Thay's neighbors' borders by raiding villages to capture slaves to work in Thayan mines. Seeking some advantage in this relationship, Thesk founded Echo Village as a trade outpost in hopes that establishing commerce with Thay.

In the, the tensions between Thay and Netheril flared and the Netherese enclave of Kolthunral arrived in the area in a surprise attack to deny the Thayans access to the Shadowfell Road, which they were using to further their ambitions at a Dread Ring across Faerûn near Neverwinter. Over the following months, the flying city unleashed a bombardment that decimated a large swath of the land, forcing Thay onto the defensive at both Surcross and Veil.

Notable Locations
Geographic Features
 The Charnel Fields, a vast plain of death and destruction west of Surcross created by the bombardment of the flying city Kolthunral. Any corpse left on or in the ground within  of this area was absorbed into the soil to reemerge in this gruesome place.
 The Shadow Veil, a massive and stable rift between the Material Plane and the Shadowfell that extended across the landscape near the town of Veil.

Settlements
 Echo Village, a Theskian trading outpost that was butchered by Thayan forces.
 Nethentir, a Thayan fort as of the late 15 century DR.
 Nethjet, a Thayan fort as of the late 15 century DR.
 Relsforin, the lone village to endure within the Charnel Fields.
 Surcross, a Thayan fort and largest of the communities in the Bonedark Hills as of the late 15 century DR.
 Veil, a Thayan-occupied village named for the Shadow Veil, which passed through the heart of town.

Inhabitants
The residents of the area lived mostly in very small and scattered communities.

As a border region between Thesk and imperialistic Thay, the Bonedark Hills were patrolled by murderous Thayan soldiers (both living and all manner of undead), including gnolls and orcs. These patrols sometimes rode skeletal steeds or made use of chariots—both heavy vehicles pulled by undead beasts as well as lighter ones that propelled themselves by magic.

Notable Inhabitants
 Dempharis Sibront, a Red Wizard enemy of Szass Tam and self-proclaimed "zulkir in exile" who sought to subvert Thayan authority in the area.
 Eyeloe, an imp in service to Szass Tam who laired in a cave within the region.
 Renault Abrecht, the commander of the Thayan forces at Veil as of 1479 DR.
 Ukulsid, the commander of the Thayan forces at Surcross as of 1479 DR.

## Borage

Borage was a typing of flowering herb found in the Realms.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold borage under its "Aurora's Larder" section for 2 copper pieces per .

Usages
 These plants could be used medicinally to treat coughs and various lung infections.
 These plants were a common form of seasoning in the Realms.
 All illithids had an allergy to borage. Like other vegetation that illithids had known allergies for, borage could cause nausea, a strong desire to withdraw from others, and if consumed in large quantities could induce debilitatingly painful internal cramps and convulsions.

## Book Brigade

The Book Brigade was a book club organized by a hin woman named Morgan in the city of Baldur's Gate, active in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, Morgan ran one of the Book Brigade's monthly gatherings to discuss a new pulpy book they had all read since the previous meeting of the club. The group met at Bloomridge Park in the Lower City and consisted of Nansi Gretta, the proprietor of the Bibliophile, half-elf Caerdwyn, and gnome Kepli for whom Nansi Gretta had feelings. At some point during the Absolute crisis of 1492 DR, Nansi Gretta chose to leave the group, owing to the busy schedule running her bookstore, yet Morgan still tried to entice the shopkeep to attend the meetings, using her affections for Kepli as an incentive. One of the works the group read was a series of smutty  books called Dusk's Dark Desires, featuring a werecow character, supposedly beautifully written. The book club was in session in Bloomridge Park when a group of assassin worshipers of Bhaal attempted to slaughter the park-goers in the middle of a day to venerate their monstrous deity. It was unknown if the Book Brigadiers survived.

## Bottle

Bottles were small liquid containers, generally larger than vials and flasks, though the terms were sometimes used interchangeably. Skins and canteens served a similar function, though usually just for water, while beakers were used for precise alchemical purposes.

An idealized average bottle could hold  of liquid. They were commonly made of glass or ceramic and stoppered with wax, leather, or cork.

Usage
Bottles were commonly used for valuable drinks like wine and potions.

## Boxes of delightful transport

Boxes of delightful transport were pairs of magical containers that could be used to transport non-magical objects between them from far distances. They were most often utilized by thieves to send messages between one another.

Description
Each box of transport was a wooden cube, measuring, and often decorated in great detail.

Powers
After placing an object in one of a pair of boxes of delightful transport and closing its lid, all one had to do was speak its command word and the object would be magically transported to its matching box. This power could be activated up to three times per day. There was no distance limit between the boxes, though they could not transport objects between different planes of existence and there was always a 5% chance that the object would not reach its matching box, instead ending up somewhere else within .

Boxes of delightful transport could not function with anything living, nor any object that was magical in nature or enspelled, such as by Nystul's magic aura.

Notable Owners
 Misti Morgan, a thief who operated in Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR.

## Book of Lairs II

Book of Lairs II is a 1-edition Dungeons & Dragons accessory. The second installment in the "Book of Lairs" format, it presents 70 small adventure scenarios by twenty different authors, including four by creator of the Forgotten Realms setting Ed Greenwood. Each is focused on a creature from either core AD&D or the Oriental Adventures accessory, with at least one explicitly stated as being in the Kara-Tur setting.

## Bowl of watery death

A bowl of watery death was a cursed magical item that drowned wizards who used it.

Description
Indistinguishable from a bowl of commanding water elementals, the bowl of watery death was a bowl  across, half that much deep, made of semi-precious stones such as malachite, lapis lazuli, or sometimes jade.

Powers
When a wizard filled the bowl with water, it responded by shrinking them to the size of an ant and plunging them into its depths, where it would immediately begin to drown them. The target was magically prevented from leaving the bowl or stopping their drowning; only a few magics, such as enlarge, animal growth or wish could rescue the victim. Dropping a potion of growth into the bowl also saved the target, while a potion of sweet water allowed the victim another opportunity to thwart the power of the bowl. Victims drowned by the bowl were irrevocably gone, even to wish spells.

If used in the Nine Hells, the subject was raised as a lemure within 10 hours.

Saltwater only made the magic more aggressive.

History
One such bowl was the only enchanted item among the wares in an abandoned shop selling grotesque-looking bowls in the Imaskari ruins underneath Solon in the . Similar magic was used in one of the trials of Red Mountain Monastery.

These devices were known in Zakhara.

## Bottomless potion of swagger

A bottomless potion of swagger was a self-refilling potion that likely fell into the possession of several adventurers in the late 15 century DR.

Description
The potion was wine-red in color. It came in a common-looking glass flask.

Powers
It allowed its user to increase the damage dealt by their successful attacks and to heal minor wounds. Drinking the potion created the feeling of being unstoppable. Some time had to pass before the potion refilled itself.

History
In the late 15 century DR, these items could be found in Neverwinter, Gauntlgrym, Mount Hotenow, and Neverwinter Wood. They were also available for purchase at shops in those locations. Such potions were likely found by the adventurers that stopped Deathsleep, the green dragon.

## Bowser

Bowser was a passionate dwarvish "alesmith" from the city of Ravens Bluff in mid-to-late 14 century DR, who sought to create perfect ale.

Description
Bowser was a highly intelligent man who could talk about his alecraft all day, every day. He was .

Possessions
Apart from his brewing work, Bowser was a capable thief and a fighter, who wielded a battle axe in battle.

By 1370 DR, Bowser developed twelve varieties of ale: Appleberry Ale (made with apples and strawberries), Blueberry Ale (blueberries and hops), Herb Ale, (a hundred different herbs), Gopher Ale (prepared by dyrrying it underground), Corn and Wheat Ale (not surprisingly made with corn and wheat), Grandmother's Ale (fermented for an entire year), Abyssmal Ale (made with abyssmal ant "milk"), Honey Wheat Ale (deep red in color made with honey), Seaweed Ale (made with seaweed), Deadman's Ale (did not actually kill anyone), Crystal Ale (fermented with enchanted crystals), and Rock Strained Ale (made by straining the brew through rocks).

History
In the, Bowser attended the year-end celebration festival in the town of Mossbridges in the Vast. The alesmith took his first attempt at winemaking – Dark Maiden Wine, to the wine-tasting and grading competition. Even though the completion rules dictated Bowser could only bring one variety of wine, they said nothing about ale, so the dwarf brought all twelve types of ales of his making.

## Bombasto

Bombasto was a barbarian patron of the Elfsong Tavern from the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Bombasto was a middle-aged human man with a tanned skin tone. He had blue eyes, a bushy braided light brown beard, and a clean-shaven head. His face and scalp were decorated with simple geometric lines and designed tattoos. He wore a simple set of barbarian clothes and a massive greataxe.

Posessions
Bombasto carried a journal written in large and loose handwriting, collecting memories of Bombasto's adventuring and juvenile pranks.

Relationships
Bombasto's traveling companion was Dame Guisarme, with whom he seemed to have a flirtatious and friendly-antagonistic relationship. She thought the barbarian's schemes and ideas were crazy but ended up getting involved in them anyway, but not before receiving a compliment from Bombasto and responding to it with a slap.

History
In the, Bombasto was a frequent customer of the Elfsong Tavern in the Eastway neighborhood of Baldur's Gate. He was often seen dining or enjoying drinks with his mercenary adventuring companion, Dame Guisarme.

One of Bombasto's adventures was his and Dame Guisarme's stand against hobgoblin raiders who held a wagonload of beer. Bombasto was thirsty and suggested the adventurers trade a wagonload of donkey shit for the hobgoblins' beer. Despite Dame Guisarme considering the idea crazy, Bombasto went on pushing a wagon of manure off a hill. It rolled down and pushed the hobgoblins' cart of beer forward, slaying in its place. Guisarme grabbed the beer wagon down in a ravine.

During their time at the Elfsong Tavern, Bombasto and Guisarme contemplated accepting a job in East Chult, guarding extractive safari out of Fort Beluarian with bonuses paid to all survivors.

## Boreal Ball

The Boreal Ball was an unending party hosted by the aptly named Baron of the Boreal Ball.

Description
The Ball was a chance for revelry and intrigue in equal measure. Guests could dance, watch the dancing, or mingle with the various fey and other attendees. Guests jockeyed to make positive impressions on each other, or to discredit their rivals, hoping to earn the Baron's favor.

Guests for the Ball received magical invitations from the Baron, which let them know the appointed time at which they were to attend. At that time, they were then teleported to the party where they danced, schemed, or did whatever else they wished for three dances before the Baron arrived to greet them.

Location
The ball was held at a palace of ice located within the Feywild.

History
Notable attendees of the Ball included the satyr Cannifer, the drow Raxas Albrethin, the dryad Darisis, the pixie Fidget, the green hag Granny Snailtongue, and the hobgoblin Varka.

At some point, Minsc (and Boo) and Delina attended the Ball.

History

Agarta the Breaker of the Night Brigade gang operated a series of arenas where beasts and prisoners were pitted against each other. Among the willing and unwilling gladiators was Bonegnasher and his band of orcs, formerly of the Storm Horns mountains. They were strong, capable fighters, but their time in the prison ended when the Riverside Rats gang sent a team into the prison to fight the Night Brigade, meeting and defeating Bonegnasher's group.The implication may be that Bonegnasher was one of the orcs that served Archwizard Amskar in Shadowfell Conspiracy, making him part of the forces of Thultanthar and thus worthy of being placed in Wheloon Prison.

## Body weaponry

Body weaponry was a psionic devotion of the psychometabolism discipline that allowed a manifester to turn one of their arms into an approximation of various common forms of weapons.

Effects
By altering the molecules in their body, a manifester transformed their arms into the form of a weapon, changing its molecular makeup into either metal, wood, bone, stone, or a combination of metal and wood. While transformed the arm would behave in every respect like the weapon it was approximating. The manifester could not transform their arm into any weapon they weren't proficient in fighting with, nor could they transform it into a ranged weapon, such as a bow or crossbow.

Prior to the Time of Troubles,The loss and stabilization of psionics mentioned in Forgotten Realms Adventures and The Complete Psionics Handbook are in-universe references to the initial lack of psionics in 2 edition AD&D, and the reintroduction of psionics with a new ruleset, respectively. Though this in-universe explanation is somewhat contradicted by the use of psionics set in Toril's past, such as Netheril: Empire of Magic. this power not only allowed a manifester to mimic a weapon, but armor as well. The type of weapon depended upon the manifester's degree of mastery with the devotion and their psionic powers as a whole, whilst the degree of armor depended upon these factors as well as whether the manifester was a cleric, fighter, or thief. The devotion operated as follows:
Level of MasteryWeapon EquivalentFirst DegreeClubSecond DegreeDaggerThird DegreeHand axeFourth DegreeMaceFifth DegreeBattle axeSixth DegreeMorningstar, as wielded by a clericSeventh DegreeBroad swordEighth DegreeLong swordNinth Degree+1 long sword, as wielded by a thiefTenth Degree+2 long swordEleventh Degree+3 long swordTwelfth Degree+4 long sword

The various benefits of this old form of body weaponry could not be used in conjunction with an actual weapon or suit of armor.

Significance
Being that it was a power that manipulated the body's molecules, body weaponry could not be manifested while imprisoned by a phase trap.

Users
This power was knowable by psionicists and wild talents. It was particularly common as a wild talent among necromancers, whom were known for developing unusual mental abilities.

In psionics terms, body weaponry was known by charonadaemons, planetars, and solars.

Tessadyle robes, unique psionically active clothing developed by illithids, conferred the ability to manifest body weaponry once per day.

Known/Notable Users
 Those possessed by Bane, acting as ersatz avatars, were said to have a power similar to body weaponry, often warping their hands into fierce talons.

## Boots of fleet and fortune

The boots of fleet and fortune were a pair of boots found in Myth Drannor during the late 14 century DR.

Accurate to their name, the boots made the wearer extremely nimble, dextrous, and above all lucky.

The boots were originally brought to Myth Drannor by an adventurer who thought it a source of easy treasure, only to fall to a terrible fate. It may have been found by the Cormanthor Elves during the Elven Crusade when they put out the fires at the Burning Library at the behest of the dragon Jurin'ildren and the elf Kaylie.

## Boots of uncanny mending

The boots of uncanny mending were a pair of boots found in Myth Drannor during the late 14 century DR.

The boots had space for holding small artisan's tools in their sides, and together they gave the wearer an exceptional aptitude for mending, building, and repairing.

The boots ended up in Myth Drannor's Temple to Labelas Enoreth, where they may have been party to some divinely-inspired time travel before being found by the Cormanthor Elves during the Elven Crusade.

## Bow of the Silver Flame

The bow of the Silver Flame was a holy longbow found in Eberron during the mid-1480s DR of Faerûn.

Description
The bow was made of a dark, near-black wood.

Powers
The bow was filled with holy and good energy, making it exceptional at smiting evil. It was even more deadly against the undead, and each arrow was magically enhanced as if they were made of silver, letting them better harm lycanthropes and vampires, and further altered so that they struck with crushing rather than piercing force, shattering skeletons easily.

History
The bow was created for the Templars of the Silver Flame, who sought a more powerful weapon for smiting the undead. The bow was later given to adventurers in Stormreach who had helped the paladin Felnis investigate the Lords of Dust when they opened a passage to Khyber and the Spinner's Prison, ultimately leading them to Faerûn.

Borgo had pallid skin and a shaven head with extensive tattoos. He wore a suit of black plate armor and carried a pair of flaming curved blades.

Borgo served the lich Droic in his headquarters at Castle Cormanthor during their raid on Myth Drannor. He may have fallen in battle defending the base when the Elves of Cormanthor attacked the Red Wizards, destroying Droic and his phylactery.

## Bohemund the Belligerent

Bohemund the Belligerent was a wizard and a street entertainer from the city of Westgate in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Bohemund was a human man with sharp facial features, a light skin tone, short black hair, and a thin black mustache. He wore striking red magely garb.

History

In the, Bohemund the Belligerent entertained passersby on the Market Triangle's central plaza with displays of magic. However, it did not impress the locals, who thought that even a well-trained monkey could do Bohemund's magic. In response, Bohemund challenged the hecklers to perform the same magic, and when the hecklers couldn't, he stated them to be below the level of a well-trained monkey.

## Boots of reflexes

Boots of reflexes were enchanted boots that originated from the nation of Amn and were designed to be worn by its notorious Shadow Thieves sometime before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
Boots of reflexes, to no surprise, improved the wearer's reflexes, allowing them to dodge out of the harm's way in mere moments, or survive an onslaught of powerful magics. Boots of reflexes were created with different enchantment strengths, from light magic that slightly honed the wearer's reflexes, to a significantly more powerful version.

History
Boots of reflexes were created long before the  to counter the elaborate deadly traps placed in place by the rich and powerful of Amn, to safeguard their riches. These magic boots ended up quickening the reactions of the Shadow Thieves best assassins and burglars.

By the late 15 century DR, many of these boots ended up in the hands of merchants of magical goods and adventurers in Northwest Faerûn and as far from Amn as the city of Westgate on the Dragon Coast.

## Boneyard (Sschindylryn)

The Boneyard was a charnel cave and killing field on the outskirts of the drow city of Sschindylryn.

Description
The Boneyard was a large cavern, the floor of which was covered with the haphazardly-scattered remains of dead mortals, from the fresh and bloody to the ancient and dusty. It was used as a kind of graveyard by Sschindylryn, specifically a convenient place to dump the remains of fallen drow—slaves, prisoners, and others who didn't warrant a proper burial. Its true purpose however was that of an outer line of defense for the city, as the massed bones and flesh made an ideal source of material for drow necromancers to animate on the spot and send at invaders. This was aided by the presence of at least one sacrificial altar used for more formal rituals.

Geography
The cavern was located near a primary portal that led directly into Sschindylryn, adjoining several more traditional drow fortifications that faced out into the wider Underdark.

Inhabitants
At any moment the Boneyard might be "home" to any amount of shambling zombies and skeletons, either left to their own devices or being led along by drow necromancers. During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, human undead joined the ranks of the defenders, drawn from the common folk of Eveningstar as well as Purple Dragons and War Wizards. Arch-Necromancer Phinrae Szind looked after the area, joined by a team of other grand necromancers and their minions.

## Borgol

Borgol was a male cyclops in service to the fomorians of the Underdark beneath the island of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles during the late 1470s DR. He was killed by drow in service to Prince Araithe of Sarifal.

Personality
He was fiercely devoted to his fomorian masters, whom he regarded as akin to gods.

History
During the late 1470s DR, Borgol was made to serve the leShay Prince Araithe and his drow troops as part of a concession relating to the Pact of Eschatos, negotiated by the leShay and the fomorians after Araithe captured a fomorian princess, Marabaldia. Araithe planned to eliminate Marabaldia without risking himself or his drow by compelling the princess's friend, Suka, to kill her, thus inciting Borgol to kill Suka. This plan failed, and Borgol fought alongside Marabaldia and Suka to escape. Borgol would ultimately lose his life in the ruins of Caer Corwell when he used his body to block drow arrows intended for Marabaldia.

Description
The boots were made of supple leather.

The boots of the woodsman let the wearer move with impressive speed, hide more easily in the shadows, and launch more devastating ambushes—making them as swift and silent as the falling night.

These boots were granted to the woodsmen who served the Crown of Cormyr and aided the druids of the King's Forest. Arcton Greenwort supplied these to heroes during the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar.

## Book of War

The Book of War was a book on military tactics known in Shou Lung.

Scholarship
Considered a classic of Shou literature, the Book of War was required reading for applicants to the bureaucracy. The annual Civil Service Examination held at Ping Chow saw would-be officials tested on their knowledge of the Book of War, the Book of Heaven, and the Histories and other works over ten hours.

Appendix
Background
The Book of War may be based on The Art of War, attributed to Sun Tzu. It is also possibly the same work as the Imaskari Book of War, given the Imaskari successor state Anok-Imaskar is the precursor of Shou Lung.

 Imaskari Book of War

## Body equilibrium

Body equilibrium was a psionic devotion of the psychometabolism discipline that allowed the psionicist to mitigate the effects of their own weight.

Effects
Through a successful use, the manifester would effectively reduce their weight to correspond to a surface, allowing them to walk on liquids, or eliminate damage from falling into a solid surface. For this reason, it was often likened to feather fall. However, it only slowed their fall through the air to  per second, and in addition allowed a psionicist to make themselves light enough to be blown away by a gust, an effect unlike that of the spell. Early reports spoke of mishaps that caused the psionicist to become dramatically heavier instead, remaining so until they managed to reverse the effect.

It would not be of much help in walking unscathed over the Lethe river of Nessus, or the rivers of fire of Phlegethos, or the lake of Cocytus, or the river Styx of Hades.

Descriptions post-dating the Year of Wild Magic, 1372 DR, instead claimed it only mitigated damage from falls partly, and that walking on any surface was only possible at a restricted, yet brisk, pace.

Notable Users
The ability was extremely common, existing even as a wild talent. Elminster in particular displayed this power as one of the many he'd been born with. Some psionicists known to wield this power included Teliene Coloara, and many yuan-ti such as Sysalok or the ancient masters.

It was also known to be employed by aratha, lythlyx, brain spiders, illithids, alhoon, ulitharids, neothelids, elder brains, intellect devourers, baku (such as Peter Thrum), topaz dragons and amethyst dragons (such as Eldenser) planetars, solars, Charon, Anthraxus, Lolth, and Bane.

## Bolok

Bolok was a domesticated bear, formerly belonging to a group of gypsies, that lived in the Kingdom of Mertwig around the 14 century DR.

History
When King Mertwig was on the verge of death at age 65, various fortune hunters and individuals claiming to be his long-lost heirs scoured his kingdom in search of wealth and treasure. During their journey to claim the throne, the would-be heirs of King Mertwig met Bolok and purchased him from his owners for 15 gold pieces to join them on their adventure.

## Borlan

Borlan was a human commoner inhabitant of Neverwinter's Beggar's Nest in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Borlan was a burly middle-aged human with a light skin tone and long blonde hair. He appeared distraught and haggard after losing his home to the plague.

History
During the time the Wailing Death ravaged Neverwinter in the, Borlan's home district, Beggar's Nest, was hit the hardest. Those who died of the plague were burned on the city streets, and, to make things worse, the district erupted in a zombie infestation. Borlan and several other refugees managed to evacuate from the district before the Neverwintran militia quarantined it off. Now homeless, Borlan had to sleep on city streets by a beggars' campfire in the City Core, where he mourned his old home and that there was not a single brave and strong hero capable of freeing the district from the undead.

Appearances

## Boarfish

Boarfish was a species of deadly poisonous fish, if consumed uncooked, found in the Nelanther Isles of the Sea of Swords.

Description
Boarfish received its name from the fact that its cooked flesh tasted a lot like bacon.

Ecology
Boarfish was considered a snack prepared in the wilderness, rather than a full dish. When preparing, the fish was halved crosswise and grilled up over hot coals, allowing it to simmer in its own juices, until the skin was blistered and began to fall off. It was served with a sauce of sourmilk, mint, and cucumber. According to Ed of the Greenwood, cooked boarfish tasted like "Spahamm."

Description
Circa 1485 DR, Bobbish was an elderly man with white hair.

History
In the mid-1480s DR, Bobbish was a local who lived near Beckside Castle, and used the bridge over the Starwater tributary as a fishing spot. He was unperturbed by the drama occurring nearby.

## Borial

Borial was an assassin with the School of Stealth in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
Borial mistrusted Count Kelmar Dargren, and was disturbed at his obsessions.

Activities
He was an assassin.

Relationships
His loyalty lay with the School of Stealth; one of his comrades was called Faerdral.

History
In Flamerule of the, Borial was hired from the School of Stealth to accompany Count Kelmar Dargren, who was obsessively investigating a series of murders. However, one evening, about a tenday from the murders starting, he was himself killed while searching for the killer in the area between Ivory Street (Zazesspur) and Temple Street.

## Bolgor

Bolgor was a tiny nation of the Whamite Isles in the Sea of Fallen Stars. It participated in a series of wild conflicts against the other nations and pirates of the Whamite Isles at some point prior to the mass death of the population in the .

Description
Bolgor had a variety of colorful political figures that vied for control of the nation and of the Whamite Isles at large. Known individuals were the Czar Ferdinand, the Princess Annya, the Prince Nicholas, and Disputin. They were served by the Imperial Army, the Imperial Air Corps, and the Gruusboatmen. The population of Bolgor included the Serfs, who although humble, participated in the wars of the Whamite isles.

Bolgor was landlocked by the Cold Desert, Veldergauttland, Penbroc, Graubünden, and Inter Mierionydd. The River Gruus ran from the mountain range which formed the border with the Cold Desert and Veldergauttland, itself forming the border with Penbroc. The historic fortress of Gruuscastle stood on the border between Bolgor, Penbroc, and Graubünden, and the three nations often fought over it.

Bolgor was the only of nations of the Whamites to have no sea border. It compensated for this with the Imperial Air Corps, which included the only airships of the archipelago.

The boots of the willed were created as a reward for the adventurers who managed to slay the great and fearsome Tyrant of Omu, one of the powerful beasts that dwelt within the Jungles of Chult.

## Boots of drafting

The boots of drafting was a pair of enchanted boots from the Chultan city of Port Nyanzaru ceated in or before the late 15 century DR.

Powers
The wearer of these boots received the boon of increased running speed and the ability to dash forward with blazing speed. Additionally to the speed, the boots slightly boosted the wearer's defense, vitality, and physical power.

## Bonesnapper (orb)

Bonesnappers and their more powerful variant, exalted Bonesnappers, were a variety of artifact orb created to be used by wizard adventurers in the Chultan city of Port Nyanzaru in the late 15 century DR.

Description
These Bonesnappers were testaments of their owners' strength and the skills of the master weaponcrafters of Chult. Those heroes who survived the deadly dangers of the jungles were honored with these enchanted wizardly orbs, as well as amulets that boosted their magics called Bonepickers.

Powers
A Bonesnapper could be enchanted to hold various additional magics custom for each owner, in addition to boosting the damage inflicted by the owner in combat, accuracy, and their ability to land well-aimed and lucky attacks against the enemies. When a Bonesnapper was wielded alongside a Bonepicker amulet, both items boosted each other's magical powers, increasing the wielder's outgoing damage, magical and physical, as well as boosting the wielder's healing magics and skills.

History
Bonesnappers were created in the late 15 century DR, and their design suggested the items' origins to be tied to the ancient Lost City of Omu.

Appendix
Appearances

References
